There is no {@{partial time derivative with respect to time multiplied by a time increment}@}, since this is {@{a virtual displacement}@}, one along {@{the constraints in an _instant_ of time}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:08:54.914Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:08:54.914Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:08.034Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:08.034Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:41:26.996Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:41:26.996Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:37:14.784Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:37:14.784Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:54:02.820Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:54:02.820Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:30:44.288Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:30:44.288Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:10:49.734Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:10:49.734Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:09.910Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:09.910Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:59.869Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:59.869Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:45:48.721Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:45:48.721Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T04:28:26.705Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T04:28:26.705Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:51.514Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:51.514Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:27:48.015Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:27:48.015Z-->

{@{The first term in D'Alembert's principle}@} above is {@{the virtual work done by the non-constraint forces __N__<sub>_k_</sub> along the virtual displacements _δ_<!-- markdown separator -->__r__<sub>_k_</sub>}@}, and can {@{without loss of generality be converted into the generalized analogues}@} by {@{the definition of [generalized forces](generalized%20force.md)}@} {@{$$Q_{j}=\sum _{k=1}^{N}\mathbf {N} _{k}\cdot {\frac {\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} }{\partial q_{j} } },$$}@} (annotation: With some {@{abuse of notation to help with understanding}@}, the above can be rewritten as {@{the much more suggestive $Q_j \, \delta q_j = \sum_{k = 1}^N \mathbf N_{k} \cdot {\delta \mathbf r}_k$}@}.) so that {@{$$\sum _{k=1}^{N}\mathbf {N} _{k}\cdot \delta \mathbf {r} _{k}=\sum _{k=1}^{N}\mathbf {N} _{k}\cdot \sum _{j=1}^{n}{\frac {\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} }{\partial q_{j} } }\delta q_{j}=\sum _{j=1}^{n}Q_{j}\delta q_{j}.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:07:15.439Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:07:15.439Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:28.996Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:28.996Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:22.031Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:22.031Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:48:22.170Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:48:22.170Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:14.183Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:14.183Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:31:36.937Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:31:36.937Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:28:52.079Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:28:52.079Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:05.925Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:05.925Z-->

{@{This (annotation: the conversion of force to generalized force)}@} is {@{half of the conversion to generalized coordinates}@}. It remains to convert {@{the acceleration term into generalized coordinates}@}, which is not {@{immediately obvious}@}. Recalling {@{the Lagrange form of Newton's second law}@}, {@{the partial derivatives of the kinetic energy}@} with respect to {@{the generalized coordinates and velocities}@} can be found to {@{give the desired result}@}:<sup>[\[9\]](#^ref-9)</sup> {@{$$\sum _{k=1}^{N}m_{k}\mathbf {a} _{k}\cdot {\frac {\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} }{\partial q_{j} } }={\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial T}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }-{\frac {\partial T}{\partial q_{j} } }.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:42:51.287Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:42:51.287Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:14.755Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:14.755Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:49.577Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:49.577Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:43:55.400Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:43:55.400Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:37.172Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:37.172Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:19.826Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:19.826Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:30.914Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:30.914Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:30:59.098Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:30:59.098Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:18:07.758Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:18:07.758Z-->

Now {@{D'Alembert's principle}@} is in {@{the generalized coordinates as required}@}, {@{$$\sum _{j=1}^{n}\left[Q_{j}-\left({\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial T}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }-{\frac {\partial T}{\partial q_{j} } }\right)\right]\delta q_{j}=0,$$}@} and since {@{these virtual displacements _δq_<sub>_j_</sub> are independent and nonzero (annotation: i.e. can be set arbitrarily)}@}, {@{the coefficients can be equated to zero}@}, resulting in {@{__Lagrange's equations__<sup>[\[25\]](#^ref-25)</sup><sup>[\[26\]](#^ref-26)</sup> or the __generalized equations of motion__}@},<sup>[\[27\]](#^ref-27)</sup> {@{$$Q_{j}={\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial T}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }-{\frac {\partial T}{\partial q_{j} } }$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:01:49.874Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:01:49.874Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:42.278Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:42.278Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:50.100Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:50.100Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:50:03.876Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:50:03.876Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:16.338Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:16.338Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:01:00.504Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:01:00.504Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:21:39.092Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:21:39.092Z-->

{@{These equations (annotation: _Lagrange's equations_ or the _generalized equations of motion_)}@} are equivalent to {@{Newton's laws _for the non-constraint forces_}@}. {@{The generalized forces in this equation}@} are derived from {@{the non-constraint forces only}@} – {@{the constraint forces}@} have been {@{excluded from D'Alembert's principle and do not need to be found}@}. {@{The generalized forces}@} may be {@{non-conservative}@}, provided they {@{satisfy D'Alembert's principle}@}.<sup>[\[28\]](#^ref-28)</sup> <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:54.780Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:54.780Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:06:53.713Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:06:53.713Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:27:47.628Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:27:47.628Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:34:48.854Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:34:48.854Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:44.224Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:44.224Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:21.396Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:21.396Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:36:04.440Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:36:04.440Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:06:55.723Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:06:55.723Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:18.725Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:18.725Z-->

### Euler–Lagrange equations and Hamilton's principle

> {@{![Least action principle in configuration space, bold q is configuration vector \(tuple of generalized coordinates\).](../../archives/Wikimedia%20Commons/Least%20action%20principle.svg)}@}
>
> As {@{the system evolves}@}, {@{__q__}@} traces {@{a path through [configuration space](configuration%20space%20(physics).md) \(only some are shown\)}@}. {@{The path taken by the system \(red\)}@} has {@{a stationary action \(δ<!-- markdown separator -->_S_ = 0\)}@} under {@{small changes in the configuration of the system \(δ<!-- markdown separator -->__q__\)}@}.<sup>[\[29\]](#^ref-29)</sup> <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:58.163Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:58.163Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:40.424Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:40.424Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:17.897Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:17.897Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:30.150Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:30.150Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:07.751Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:07.751Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:25.415Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:25.415Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:30.719Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:30.719Z-->

For {@{a non-conservative force which depends on velocity}@}, it _may_ be {@{possible to find a potential energy function _V_ that depends on positions and velocities}@}. (annotation: For {@{a conservative force}@}, by {@{definition there is a potential energy function such that $\nabla V(\mathbf r) = -\mathbf F$}@}, and this is {@{invariant under any smooth, invertible coordinate transformation}@}, so {@{$\nabla V(\mathbf q) = -\mathbf Q$ or equivalently $Q_j = -\frac {\partial V} {\partial q_j}$ where $Q_j$ is in _covariant_ form}@}.) If {@{the generalized forces _Q_<sub>_i_</sub> can be derived from a potential _V_}@} such that<sup>[\[30\]](#^ref-30)</sup><sup>[\[31\]](#^ref-31)</sup> {@{$$Q_{j}={\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial V}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }-{\frac {\partial V}{\partial q_{j} } },$$}@} equating to {@{Lagrange's equations}@} (annotation: Note for {@{a conservative force}@}, {@{$Q_j = -\frac {\partial V} {\partial q_j}$ where $Q_j$ is in _covariant_ form}@}, and {@{$V$ does not depend on the velocities}@}, so {@{the other term is always zero}@} and thus it also {@{satisfies Lagrange's equations}@}.) and defining {@{the Lagrangian as _L_ = _T_ − _V_}@} obtains {@{__Lagrange's equations of the second kind__ or the __Euler–Lagrange equations__ of motion}@} {@{$${\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{j} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }=0.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:08:53.898Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:08:53.898Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:13:12.619Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:13:12.619Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:35:08.398Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:35:08.398Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:00:06.653Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:00:06.653Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:56.033Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:56.033Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:32:48.508Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:32:48.508Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T05:59:54.579Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T05:59:54.579Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:25:50.302Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:25:50.302Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:29.231Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:29.231Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:27:44.656Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:27:44.656Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:14.425Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:14.425Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:16.902Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:16.902Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:57.199Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:57.199Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:25:23.196Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:25:23.196Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:34.561Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:34.561Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:47.078Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:47.078Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:16.720Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:16.720Z-->

However, {@{the Euler–Lagrange equations}@} can only account for {@{non-conservative forces _if_ a potential can be found as shown}@}. (annotation: For {@{a conservative force}@}, this is {@{always possible}@}.) This may not {@{always be possible for non-conservative forces}@}, and {@{Lagrange's equations (annotation: the equations linking only $T$ and $Q_j$, not $V$)}@} do not {@{involve any potential, only generalized forces}@}; therefore they are {@{more general than the Euler–Lagrange equations (annotation: the equations linking $T$ and $V$ only, not $Q_j$)}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:52.755Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:52.755Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:36:56.992Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:36:56.992Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:09:09.793Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:09:09.793Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:07:30.212Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:07:30.212Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:25:52.308Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:25:52.308Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:15.258Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:15.258Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:06:50.113Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:06:50.113Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:01:45.357Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:01:45.357Z-->

{@{The Euler–Lagrange equations}@} also follow from {@{the [calculus of variations](calculus%20of%20variations.md) (annotation: another approach to obtain the equations)}@}. {@{The _variation_ of the Lagrangian}@} is {@{$$\delta L=\sum _{j=1}^{n}\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{j} } }\delta q_{j}+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }\delta {\dot {q} }_{j}\right),\quad \delta {\dot {q} }_{j}\equiv \delta {\frac {\mathrm {d} q_{j} }{\mathrm {d} t} }\equiv {\frac {\mathrm {d} (\delta q_{j})}{\mathrm {d} t} },$$}@} (annotation: For {@{the same reason as above}@}, since {@{the _variation_ is defined at a _fixed instant_ of time}@}, {@{the time partial derivative does not appear}@}.) which has {@{a form similar to the [total differential](total%20differential.md#differentials%20in%20several%20variables) of _L_}@}, but {@{the virtual displacements and their time derivatives}@} replace {@{differentials}@}, and there is no {@{time increment in accordance with the definition of the virtual displacements}@}. {@{An [integration by parts](integration%20by%20parts.md) with respect to time}@} can transfer {@{the time derivative of _δq_<sub>_j_</sub> to the ∂<!-- markdown separator -->_L_<!-- markdown separator -->/∂\(d<!-- markdown separator -->_q<sub>j</sub>_<!-- markdown separator -->/d<!-- markdown separator -->_t_\)}@}, in the process {@{exchanging d\(_δq_<sub>_j_</sub>\)/d<!-- markdown separator -->_t_ for _δq_<sub>_j_</sub>}@}, allowing {@{the independent virtual displacements to be factorized from the derivatives of the Lagrangian}@}, {@{$${\begin{aligned}\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }\delta L\,\mathrm {d} t&=\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }\sum _{j=1}^{n}\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{j} } }\delta q_{j}+{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }\delta q_{j}\right)-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }\delta q_{j}\right)\,\mathrm {d} t\\&=\sum _{j=1}^{n}\left[{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }\delta q_{j}\right]_{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }+\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }\sum _{j=1}^{n}\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{j} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }\right)\delta q_{j}\,\mathrm {d} t.\end{aligned} }$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:31:48.593Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:31:48.593Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:38:04.918Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:38:04.918Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:57.747Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:57.747Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:30:42.517Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:30:42.517Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:28:13.912Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:28:13.912Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:33:45.695Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:33:45.695Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:00:54.415Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:00:54.415Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:44:45.106Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:44:45.106Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:08:29.762Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:08:29.762Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:20.164Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:20.164Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:37:19.604Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:37:19.604Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:40:20.388Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:40:20.388Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:33:48.710Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:33:48.710Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:50.576Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:50.576Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:59.271Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:59.271Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:32:43.916Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:32:43.916Z-->

Now, if {@{the condition _δq_<sub>_j_</sub>\(_t_<sub>1</sub>\) = _δq_<sub>_j_</sub>\(_t_<sub>2</sub>\) = 0 holds for all _j_}@}, (annotation: {@{These boundary conditions}@} are {@{physically justified because competing paths}@} share {@{identical initial and final endpoint configurations at times $t_1$ and $t_2$}@}.) {@{the terms not integrated are zero}@}. If in addition {@{the entire time integral of _δL_ is zero}@}, (annotation: {@{The stationary action condition $\int \delta L \, dt = 0$}@} serves as {@{the core physical postulate}@}, asserting that {@{physical systems follow paths that extremize the integrated Lagrangian}@}. {@{Physically}@}, this postulate emerges as {@{the classical limit of quantum mechanics}@}: {@{paths away from the stationary trajectory}@} suffer {@{destructive phase interference}@}, while {@{the stationary path interferes constructively to dominate macroscopic behavior}@}.) then because {@{the _δq_<sub>_j_</sub> are independent}@}, and {@{the only way for a definite integral to be zero}@} is {@{if the integrand equals zero}@}, each of {@{the coefficients of _δq_<sub>_j_</sub> must also be zero}@}. Then we obtain {@{the equations of motion}@}. This can be summarized by {@{__[Hamilton's principle](Hamilton's%20principle.md)__}@}: {@{$$\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }\delta L\,\mathrm {d} t=0.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:50:06.387Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:50:06.387Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:26.637Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:26.637Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:28:24.693Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:28:24.693Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:56.374Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:56.374Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:29.710Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:29.710Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:50:05.115Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:50:05.115Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:18:14.072Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:18:14.072Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:52:24.571Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:52:24.571Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:14:04.511Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:14:04.511Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:38.624Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:38.624Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:40:36.902Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:40:36.902Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:06:52.547Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:06:52.547Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:54:44.837Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:54:44.837Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:37:31.690Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:37:31.690Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:17:33.517Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:17:33.517Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:14.101Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:14.101Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:32:45.940Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:32:45.940Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:53.791Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:53.791Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:31.551Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:31.551Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:13:54.566Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:13:54.566Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:41:52.254Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:41:52.254Z-->

{@{The time integral of the Lagrangian}@} is {@{another quantity called the [action](action%20(physics).md)}@}, defined as<sup>[\[32\]](#^ref-32)</sup> {@{$$S=\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }L\,\mathrm {d} t,$$}@} which is {@{a _[functional](functional%20(mathematics).md)_}@}; it takes in {@{the Lagrangian function for all times between _t_<sub>1</sub> and _t_<sub>2</sub>}@} and returns {@{a scalar value}@}. Its {@{dimensions}@} are the same as {@{\[[angular momentum](angular%20momentum.md)\], \[energy\]·\[time\], or \[length\]·\[momentum\]}@}. With {@{this definition Hamilton's principle}@} is {@{$$\delta S=0.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:39.434Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:39.434Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T05:59:50.604Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T05:59:50.604Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:39.894Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:39.894Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:39.756Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:39.756Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:09:14.188Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:09:14.188Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:16.748Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:16.748Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:07.559Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:07.559Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:39.678Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:39.678Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:11:01.190Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:11:01.190Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:31:17.527Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:31:17.527Z-->

Instead of thinking about {@{particles accelerating in response to applied forces}@}, one might think of them {@{picking out the path with a stationary action}@}, with {@{the end points of the path in configuration space held fixed at the initial and final times}@}. {@{Hamilton's principle}@} is one of {@{several [action principles](action%20principles.md)}@}.<sup>[\[33\]](#^ref-33)</sup> <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:38.140Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:38.140Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:08:31.770Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:08:31.770Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:54.578Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:54.578Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:24:25.664Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:24:25.664Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:34:41.690Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:34:41.690Z-->

Historically, the idea of finding {@{the shortest path a particle can follow subject to a force}@} motivated {@{the first applications of the [calculus of variations](calculus%20of%20variations.md) to mechanical problems}@}, such as {@{the [Brachistochrone problem](brachistochrone%20problem.md) solved by [Jean Bernoulli](Jean%20Bernoulli.md) in 1696}@}, as well as {@{[Leibniz](Gottfried%20Wilhelm%20Leibniz.md), [Daniel Bernoulli](Daniel%20Bernoulli.md), [L'Hôpital](Guillaume%20de%20l'Hôpital.md) around the same time}@}, and {@{[Newton](Isaac%20Newton.md) the following year}@}.<sup>[\[34\]](#^ref-34)</sup> {@{Newton himself}@} was thinking {@{along the lines of the variational calculus, but did not publish}@}.<sup>[\[34\]](#^ref-34)</sup> These ideas in turn lead to {@{the [variational principles](variational%20principle.md) of mechanics}@}, of {@{[Fermat](Pierre%20de%20Fermat.md), [Maupertuis](Pierre%20Louis%20Maupertuis.md), [Euler](Leonhard%20Euler.md), [Hamilton](William%20Rowan%20Hamilton.md), and others}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:54.468Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:54.468Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:44:10.610Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:44:10.610Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:17.142Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:17.142Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:16:38.678Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:16:38.678Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:33:05.822Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:33:05.822Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:08:36.850Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:08:36.850Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:40:41.306Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:40:41.306Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:14.634Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:14.634Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:31:15.356Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:31:15.356Z-->

{@{Hamilton's principle}@} can be applied to {@{[nonholonomic constraints](nonholonomic%20constraints.md)}@} if {@{the constraint equations can be put into a certain form}@}, {@{a [linear combination](linear%20combination.md) of first order differentials in the coordinates}@}. {@{The resulting constraint equation}@} can be {@{rearranged into first order differential equation}@}.<sup>[\[35\]](#^ref-35)</sup> This will not {@{be given here}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-23T04:28:27.762Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T04:28:27.762Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:29.808Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:29.808Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:37:22.146Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:37:22.146Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T04:28:29.878Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T04:28:29.878Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:23:53.121Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:23:53.121Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:52:53.023Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:52:53.023Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:47.378Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:47.378Z-->

### Lagrange multipliers and constraints

{@{The Lagrangian _L_}@} can be {@{varied in the Cartesian __r__<sub>_k_</sub> coordinates, for _N_ particles}@}, {@{$$\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }\sum _{k=1}^{N}\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {r} } }_{k} } }\right)\cdot \delta \mathbf {r} _{k}\,\mathrm {d} t=0.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:38.480Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:38.480Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:18:10.728Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:18:10.728Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:25:52.281Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:25:52.281Z-->

{@{Hamilton's principle}@} is still {@{valid even if the coordinates _L_ is expressed in are not independent}@}, here {@{__r__<sub>_k_</sub>}@}, but {@{the constraints are still assumed to be holonomic}@}.<sup>[\[36\]](#^ref-36)</sup> As always {@{the end points are fixed}@} {@{_δ_<!-- markdown separator -->__r__<sub>_k_</sub>\(_t_<sub>1</sub>\) = _δ_<!-- markdown separator -->__r__<sub>_k_</sub>\(_t_<sub>2</sub>\) = __0__ for all _k_}@}. {@{What cannot be done}@} is to simply {@{equate the coefficients of _δ_<!-- markdown separator -->__r__<sub>_k_</sub> to zero because the _δ_<!-- markdown separator -->__r__<sub>_k_</sub> are not independent}@}. Instead, {@{the method of [Lagrange multipliers](Lagrange%20multiplier.md)}@} can be used to {@{include the constraints}@}. Multiplying {@{each constraint equation _f_<sub>_i_</sub>\(__r__<sub>_k_</sub>, _t_\) = 0}@} by {@{a Lagrange multiplier _λ_<sub>_i_</sub> for _i_ = 1, 2, ..., _C_}@}, and adding {@{the results to the original Lagrangian}@}, gives {@{the new Lagrangian $$L'=L(\mathbf {r} _{1},\mathbf {r} _{2},\ldots ,{\dot {\mathbf {r} } }_{1},{\dot {\mathbf {r} } }_{2},\ldots ,t)+\sum _{i=1}^{C}\lambda _{i}(t)f_{i}(\mathbf {r} _{k},t).$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:33:15.625Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:33:15.625Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:17.482Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:17.482Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:32:51.707Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:32:51.707Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:28:52.312Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:28:52.312Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:29:14.148Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:29:14.148Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:28:24.591Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:28:24.591Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:12:16.785Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:12:16.785Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:37.751Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:37.751Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:51.282Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:51.282Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:23:51.774Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:23:51.774Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:27:59.708Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:27:59.708Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:49:54.142Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:49:54.142Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:19.010Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:19.010Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:42.522Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:42.522Z-->

{@{The Lagrange multipliers}@} are {@{arbitrary functions of time _t_, but not functions of the coordinates __r__<sub>_k_</sub>}@}, so the multipliers are {@{on equal footing with the position coordinates}@}. Varying {@{this new Lagrangian and integrating with respect to time}@} gives {@{$$\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }\delta L'\mathrm {d} t=\int _{t_{1} }^{t_{2} }\sum _{k=1}^{N}\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {r} } }_{k} } }+\sum _{i=1}^{C}\lambda _{i}{\frac {\partial f_{i} }{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }\right)\cdot \delta \mathbf {r} _{k}\,\mathrm {d} t=0.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:31:16.302Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:31:16.302Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:17:42.690Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:17:42.690Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:57:22.288Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:57:22.288Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:21:25.347Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:21:25.347Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:33.969Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:33.969Z-->

{@{The introduced multipliers}@} can be found so that {@{the coefficients of _δ_<!-- markdown separator -->__r__<sub>_k_</sub> are zero}@}, even though {@{the __r__<sub>_k_</sub> are not independent}@}. (annotation: With {@{$C$ constraints coupling the $3N$ coordinates}@}, only {@{$3N - C$ variations $\delta \mathbf{r}_k$ are independent}@}. We use {@{the $C$ free multiplier functions $\lambda_i(t)$}@} to algebraically {@{force the coefficients of the $C$ dependent variations to zero}@}, dropping {@{those terms from the integrand entirely}@}. {@{The remaining integral}@} contains {@{solely independent variations}@}, which can be {@{varied arbitrarily}@}, forcing their {@{respective coefficients to vanish by the fundamental lemma of the calculus of variations}@}. Consequently, {@{$C$ coefficients are zeroed}@} by our {@{choice of $\lambda_i(t)$}@} and {@{the remaining $3N - C$ are zeroed by coordinate independence}@}, ensuring {@{every coefficient is identically zero}@}.) {@{The equations of motion}@} follow. From {@{the preceding analysis}@}, obtaining {@{the solution to this integral}@} is equivalent to the statement {@{$${\frac {\partial L'}{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L'}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {r} } }_{k} } }=0\quad \Rightarrow \quad {\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {r} } }_{k} } }+\sum _{i=1}^{C}\lambda _{i}{\frac {\partial f_{i} }{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }=0,$$}@} which are {@{__Lagrange's equations of the first kind__}@}. Also, {@{the _λ_<sub>_i_</sub> Euler-Lagrange equations for the new Lagrangian}@} return {@{the constraint equations}@} {@{$${\frac {\partial L'}{\partial \lambda _{i} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L'}{\partial {\dot {\lambda } }_{i} } }=0\quad \Rightarrow \quad f_{i}(\mathbf {r} _{k},t)=0.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:35:09.690Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:35:09.690Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:13:24.021Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:13:24.021Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:06.605Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:06.605Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:46.456Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:46.456Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:59.472Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:59.472Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:42:20.012Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:42:20.012Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:38:58.085Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:38:58.085Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:19.835Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:19.835Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:04:02.567Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:04:02.567Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:44:25.030Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:44:25.030Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:34.881Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:34.881Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:03:52.686Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:03:52.686Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:24.326Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:24.326Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:13:55.983Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:13:55.983Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:24:39.738Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:24:39.738Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:57:23.620Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:57:23.620Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:26:03.747Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:26:03.747Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:22.427Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:22.427Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:13:26.482Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:13:26.482Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:43:23.327Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:43:23.327Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:30.189Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:30.189Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:00:09.386Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:00:09.386Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:59.237Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:59.237Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:40:27.170Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:40:27.170Z-->

For the case of {@{a conservative force given by the gradient of some potential energy _V_}@}, a function of {@{the __r__<sub>k</sub> coordinates only}@}, substituting {@{the Lagrangian _L_ = _T_ − _V_}@} gives {@{$$\underbrace { {\frac {\partial T}{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }-{\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial T}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {r} } }_{k} } } } _{-\mathbf {F} _{k} }+\underbrace {-{\frac {\partial V}{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } } } _{\mathbf {N} _{k} }+\sum _{i=1}^{C}\lambda _{i}{\frac {\partial f_{i} }{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } }=0,$$}@} and identifying {@{the derivatives of kinetic energy}@} as {@{the \(negative of the\) resultant force}@}, (annotation: They equal {@{$-F_k$ because, by d'Alembert's principle}@}, {@{the time derivative of generalized momentum $\frac{d}{dt}\frac{\partial T}{\partial \dot{r}_k}$ minus the kinetic spatial variation $\frac{\partial T}{\partial r_k}$}@} represents {@{the net resultant force $F_k$ acting along coordinate $r_k$}@}. Note in {@{the special case of Cartesian coordinates}@}, {@{$\frac {\partial T} {\partial {\mathbf r}_k} = 0$}@}, recovering {@{the familiar Newton's second law}@}.) and {@{the derivatives of the potential}@} equaling {@{the non-constraint force}@}, (annotation: It equals {@{$N_k$ by the definition of a conservative force}@}, where {@{the negative spatial gradient of potential energy yields the applied non-constraint force $N_k$}@}.) it follows {@{the constraint forces}@} are {@{$$\mathbf {C} _{k}=\sum _{i=1}^{C}\lambda _{i}{\frac {\partial f_{i} }{\partial \mathbf {r} _{k} } },$$}@} thus giving {@{the constraint forces explicitly}@} in terms of {@{the constraint equations and the Lagrange multipliers}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:37:00.281Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:37:00.281Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:00:55.273Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:00:55.273Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:59.523Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:59.523Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:11.716Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:11.716Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:50:33.843Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:50:33.843Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:09.138Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:09.138Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:40:49.991Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:40:49.991Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:11:09.556Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:11:09.556Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:43:57.076Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:43:57.076Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:31:11.297Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:31:11.297Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:35:16.308Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:35:16.308Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:25:55.859Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:25:55.859Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:50:37.583Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:50:37.583Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:45.139Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:45.139Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:41:36.987Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:41:36.987Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:28:16.250Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:28:16.250Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:28:18.020Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:28:18.020Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:08:42.643Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:08:42.643Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:40.666Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:40.666Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:37.766Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:37.766Z-->

## properties of the Lagrangian

### non-uniqueness

{@{The Lagrangian of a given system}@} is {@{not unique}@}. {@{A Lagrangian _L_}@} can be {@{multiplied by a nonzero constant _a_ and shifted by an arbitrary constant _b_}@}, and {@{the new Lagrangian _L_<!-- markdown separator -->′ = _aL_ + _b_}@} will describe {@{the same motion as _L_}@}. If one restricts {@{as above to trajectories __q__ over a given time interval \[_t_<sub>st</sub>, _t_<sub>fin</sub>\]}@} and fixed {@{end points _P_<sub>st</sub> = __q__\(_t_<sub>st</sub>\) and _P_<sub>fin</sub> = __q__\(_t_<sub>fin</sub>\)}@}, then {@{two Lagrangians describing the same system}@} can differ by {@{the "total time derivative" of a function _f_\(__q__, _t_\)}@}:<sup>[\[37\]](#^ref-37)</sup> {@{$$L'(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } },t)=L(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } },t)+{\frac {\mathrm {d} f(\mathbf {q} ,t)}{\mathrm {d} t} },$$}@} where {@{${\frac {\mathrm {d} f(\mathbf {q} ,t)}{\mathrm {d} t} }$}@} means {@{${\frac {\partial f(\mathbf {q} ,t)}{\partial t} }+\sum _{i}{\frac {\partial f(\mathbf {q} ,t)}{\partial q_{i} } }{\dot {q} }_{i}$}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:15.586Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:15.586Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:07.067Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:07.067Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:27:08.928Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:27:08.928Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:44:09.529Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:44:09.529Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:57:03.458Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:57:03.458Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:06:59.386Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:06:59.386Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:38.720Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:38.720Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:37:17.707Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:37:17.707Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:04:01.202Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:04:01.202Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:43.575Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:43.575Z!fsrs,2026-09-05T06:57:21.317Z,19,18.52175344,1,2,2,0,0,2026-08-17T06:57:21.317Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:58.493Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:58.493Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:28:02.203Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:28:02.203Z-->

Both {@{Lagrangians _L_ and _L_<!-- markdown separator -->′}@} produce {@{the same equations of motion}@}<sup>[\[38\]](#^ref-38)</sup><sup>[\[39\]](#^ref-39)</sup> since {@{the corresponding actions _S_ and _S_<!-- markdown separator -->′ are related}@} via {@{$${\begin{aligned}S'[\mathbf {q} ]&=\int _{t_{\text{st} } }^{t_{\text{fin} } }L'(\mathbf {q} (t),{\dot {\mathbf {q} } }(t),t)\,dt\\&=\int _{t_{\text{st} } }^{t_{\text{fin} } }L(\mathbf {q} (t),{\dot {\mathbf {q} } }(t),t)\,dt+\int _{t_{\text{st} } }^{t_{\text{fin} } }{\frac {\mathrm {d} f(\mathbf {q} (t),t)}{\mathrm {d} t} }\,dt\\&=S[\mathbf {q} ]+f(P_{\text{fin} },t_{\text{fin} })-f(P_{\text{st} },t_{\text{st} }),\end{aligned} }$$}@} with {@{the last two components _f_\(_P_<sub>fin</sub>, _t_<sub>fin</sub>\) and _f_\(_P_<sub>st</sub>, _t_<sub>st</sub>\)}@} {@{independent of __q__}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:33.917Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:33.917Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:57.024Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:57.024Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:09:41.219Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:09:41.219Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:49:53.099Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:49:53.099Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:55.455Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:55.455Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:13.890Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:13.890Z-->

### invariance under point transformations

Given {@{a set of generalized coordinates __q__}@}, if we change {@{these variables to a new set of generalized coordinates __Q__}@} according to {@{a [point transformation](canonical%20transformation.md) __Q__ = __Q__\(__q__, _t_\) which is invertible as __q__ = __q__\(__Q__, _t_\)}@}, {@{the new Lagrangian _L_<!-- markdown separator -->′}@} is {@{a function of the new coordinates and similarly for the constraints}@} {@{$${\begin{aligned}L'(\mathbf {Q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } },t)&=L(\mathbf {q} (\mathbf {Q} ,t),{\dot {\mathbf {q} } }(\mathbf {Q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } },t),t),\\\phi _{j}'(\mathbf {Q} ,t)&=\phi _{j}(\mathbf {q} (\mathbf {Q} ,t),t)\end{aligned} }$$}@} and by {@{the [chain rule](chain%20rule.md#higher%20dimensions) for partial differentiation}@}, Lagrange's equations are {@{invariant under this transformation}@};<sup>[\[40\]](#^ref-40)</sup><sup>\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%20needed)_\]</sup> {@{$${\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L'}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{i} } }={\frac {\partial L'}{\partial Q_{i} } }+\sum _{j}\lambda _{j}{\frac {\partial \phi '_{j} }{\partial Q_{i} } }.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:24:26.689Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:24:26.689Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:43:52.971Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:43:52.971Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:54.936Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:54.936Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:37:04.389Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:37:04.389Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:11:02.297Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:11:02.297Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:29:28.126Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:29:28.126Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:06.967Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:06.967Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:25.062Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:25.062Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:32:21.945Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:32:21.945Z-->

> __Proof__ (annotation: of {@{invariance of Lagrange's equations under point transformations}@})
>
> For {@{a coordinate transformation $Q_{i}=Q_{i}(\mathbf {q} ,t)$}@}, we have {@{$$d{Q_{i} }=\sum _{k}{\frac {\partial Q_{i} }{\partial q_{k} } }d{q_{k} }+{\frac {\partial Q_{k} }{\partial t} }dt,$$}@} which implies that {@{${\dot {Q_{i} } }=\sum _{k}{\frac {\partial Q_{i} }{\partial q_{k} } }(\mathbf {q} ,t)\,{\dot {q} }_{k}+{\frac {\partial Q_{k} }{\partial t} }(\mathbf {q} ,t)$}@} (annotation: extract {@{the $dt$ component}@}, being aware that {@{$dq_k$ contains the $dt$ component}@}) which implies that {@{${\frac {\partial {\dot {Q_{i} } } }{\partial {\dot {q} }_{k} } }={\frac {\partial Q_{i} }{\partial q_{k} } }$}@}. (annotation: This establishes {@{the "cancellation of dots"}@}, proving that {@{the velocity-space Jacobian $\frac{\partial \dot{Q}_i}{\partial \dot{q}_k}$}@} identically equals {@{the underlying configuration-space Jacobian $\frac{\partial Q_i}{\partial q_k}$}@}.)
>
> It also follows that: {@{$${\frac {\partial {\dot {Q_{i} } } }{\partial q_{j} } }=\sum _{k}{\frac {\partial ^{2}Q_{i} }{\partial q_{j}\partial q_{k} } }(\mathbf {q} ,t)\,{\dot {q} }_{k}+{\frac {\partial ^{2}Q_{k} }{\partial q_{j}\partial t} }(\mathbf {q} ,t)$$}@} and similarly: {@{$${\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial {Q_{i} } }{\partial q_{j} } }\right)=\sum _{k}{\frac {\partial ^{2}Q_{i} }{\partial q_{k}\partial q_{j} } }(\mathbf {q} ,t)\,{\dot {q} }_{k}+{\frac {\partial ^{2}Q_{k} }{\partial t\partial q_{j} } }(\mathbf {q} ,t)$$}@} which imply that {@{${\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial Q_{i} }{\partial q_{k} } }\right)={\frac {\partial {\dot {Q} }_{i} }{\partial q_{k} } }$}@}. (annotation: This demonstrates that {@{total time differentiation $\frac{d}{dt}$ commutes}@} with {@{configuration partial differentiation $\frac{\partial}{\partial q_k}$ along system trajectories}@}.) {@{The two derived relations}@} can be {@{employed in the proof}@}.
>
> (annotation: We establish {@{these identities to trade troublesome time derivatives for spatial gradients}@}, leveraging {@{a fundamental geometric reality}@}: {@{point transformations $Q=Q(q,t)$}@} reshape {@{space, not velocity space itself}@}. Because {@{coordinate maps are velocity-independent}@}, {@{boosting your speed at a fixed position (where the boost is from the transform)}@} simply {@{scales how fast you traverse the local spatial grid}@}, making {@{velocity sensitivity ($\frac{\partial \dot{Q}_i}{\partial \dot{q}_k}$) identically equal to the spatial Jacobian ($\frac{\partial Q_i}{\partial q_k}$)}@}. Meanwhile, as {@{neighboring points flow along trajectories}@}, the rate at which {@{the spatial coordinate grid stretches over time ($\frac{d}{dt}\frac{\partial Q_i}{\partial q_k}$)}@} is geometrically just {@{the velocity gradient (where the velocity represents, given a fixed point in $\mathbf q$, how the transformed point $\mathbf Q$ is moving) between adjacent points ($\frac{\partial \dot{Q}_i}{\partial q_k}$)}@}. This is {@{a direct consequence}@} of {@{trajectory flows ($\mathcal{L}_X$) commuting with spatial exterior differentiation ($d$)}@}.)
>
> Starting from {@{Euler Lagrange equations in initial set of generalized coordinates}@}, we have: {@{$${\begin{aligned}{\frac {d}{dt} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }-{\frac { {\partial }L}{\partial q_{i} } }-\sum _{j}\lambda _{j}{\frac {\partial \phi _{j} }{\partial q_{i} } }=0\\\sum _{k}\left({\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} } }{\frac {\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} }{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }\right)-{\frac { {\partial }L}{\partial Q_{k} } }{\frac {\partial Q_{k} }{\partial {q}_{i} } }-{\frac { {\partial }L}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} } }{\frac {\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} }{\partial {q}_{i} } }-\sum _{j}\lambda _{j}{\frac {\partial \phi _{j} }{\partial Q_{k} } }{\frac {\partial Q_{k} }{\partial q_{i} } }\right)=0\\\sum _{k}\left({\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} } }\right){\frac {\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} }{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} } }{\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} }{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }\right)-{\frac { {\partial }L}{\partial Q_{k} } }{\frac {\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} }{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }-{\frac { {\partial }L}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} } }{\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial Q_{k} }{\partial q_{i} } }\right)-\sum _{j}\lambda _{j}{\frac {\partial \phi _{j} }{\partial Q_{k} } }{\frac {\partial Q_{k} }{\partial q_{i} } }\right)=0\\\sum _{k}\left({\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{k} } }\right)-{\frac {\partial L}{\partial Q_{k} } }-\sum _{j}\lambda _{j}{\frac {\partial \phi _{j} }{\partial Q_{k} } }\right){\frac {\partial Q_{k} }{\partial q_{i} } }=0\\\end{aligned} }$$}@} (annotation: An obvious hint: Look at {@{the constraint term}@}. Since it is the only term that contains {@{the Lagrange multipliers $\lambda_j$}@}, it hints at {@{extracting the Jacobian $\frac{\partial Q_k}{\partial q_i}$}@} to {@{isolate the transformed Euler-Lagrange equations in the new coordinates}@}. A less obvious hint: Whenever you see {@{a total derivative of a product}@}, try applying {@{the product rule and see where it leads you to}@}.)
>
> (annotation: {@{The Euler-Lagrange expression $E_i(q) = \frac{d}{dt}\frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot{q}_i} - \frac{\partial L}{\partial q_i} - \sum_j \lambda_j \frac{\partial \phi_j}{\partial q_i}$}@} transforms {@{covariantly under a point transformation $Q = Q(q, t)$}@}, as shown by {@{the equality between the left hand side of the first and last (fourth) line}@}. To {@{remember it is covariant}@}, it involves {@{spatial derivatives $\frac{\partial}{\partial q^i}$}@}; and separately, recall that {@{contravariant displacements $\delta q$ push forward from $q$ to $Q$}@} using {@{the Jacobian $J_{ki} = \frac{\partial Q_k}{\partial q_i}$}@}, but {@{$E_i$ transforms in reverse}@} by {@{pulling back from $Q$ to $q$ through the transposed Jacobian $J^T$}@}.
>
> This yields {@{the compact relation}@}: {@{$$\mathbf{E}(q) = J^T \mathbf{E}(Q)$$}@} where {@{$J_{ki} = \frac{\partial Q_k}{\partial q_i}$ is the Jacobian matrix of the coordinate transformation}@}. Because {@{the transformation $q \to Q$ is invertible}@}, $J$ is {@{non-singular ($\det J \neq 0$)}@}, which guarantees that {@{$J^T$ is bijective with a trivial kernel}@}. Consequently, {@{$\mathbf{E}(q) = \mathbf{0}$ holds if and only if $\mathbf{E}(Q) = \mathbf{0}$}@}. This explains {@{the next paragraph's conclusion}@}.)
>
> Since {@{the transformation from $q\rightarrow Q$ is invertible}@}, it follows that {@{the form of the Euler-Lagrange equation is invariant}@} i.e., {@{$${\frac {d}{dt} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {Q} }_{i} } }-{\frac { {\partial }L}{\partial Q_{i} } }-\sum _{j}\lambda _{j}{\frac {\partial \phi _{j} }{\partial Q_{i} } }=0.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:14.892Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:14.892Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:58.055Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:58.055Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:16.312Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:16.312Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:15.291Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:15.291Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:08.947Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:08.947Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:29.870Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:29.870Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:50.338Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:50.338Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:35:18.357Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:35:18.357Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:52:52.218Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:52:52.218Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:34.346Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:34.346Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:04.242Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:04.242Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:26.739Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:26.739Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:52:51.947Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:52:51.947Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:21.921Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:21.921Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:28:47.836Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:28:47.836Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:26.068Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:26.068Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:10.009Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:10.009Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:41:09.437Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:41:09.437Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:09:14.177Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:09:14.177Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T05:59:39.290Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T05:59:39.290Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:45.305Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:45.305Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:11.351Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:11.351Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:49.782Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:49.782Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:36.811Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:36.811Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:22.507Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:22.507Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T19:07:58.582Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T19:07:58.582Z!fsrs,2026-08-21T06:57:27.076Z,4,3.94605407,1,2,2,0,0,2026-08-17T06:57:27.076Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:13:50.078Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:13:50.078Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:27.845Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:27.845Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:02.375Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:02.375Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:10:43.595Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:10:43.595Z!fsrs,2026-08-21T06:57:31.775Z,4,3.94605407,1,2,2,0,0,2026-08-17T06:57:31.775Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:24.267Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:24.267Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:24:46.320Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:24:46.320Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:27:50.473Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:27:50.473Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:37:29.989Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:37:29.989Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:33:50.431Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:33:50.431Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:35:05.883Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:35:05.883Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:18:32.549Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:18:32.549Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:28:05.570Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:28:05.570Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:42.165Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:42.165Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:03.704Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:03.704Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:44:51.427Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:44:51.427Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:55:52.324Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:55:52.324Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:45.455Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:45.455Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:18:20.162Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:18:20.162Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:11.488Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:11.488Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:28:16.794Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:28:16.794Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:39:58.740Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:39:58.740Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:31:54.828Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:31:54.828Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:25:52.759Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:25:52.759Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:35:22.950Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:35:22.950Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:40:41.685Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:40:41.685Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:41:48.956Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:41:48.956Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:46.370Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:46.370Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:37.918Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:37.918Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:52.760Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:52.760Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:40:36.021Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:40:36.021Z-->

### cyclic coordinates and conserved momenta

{@{An important property of the Lagrangian}@} is that {@{[conserved quantities](conservation%20law.md) can easily be read off from it}@}. {@{The _generalized momentum_ "canonically conjugate to" the coordinate _q<sub>i</sub>_}@} is defined by {@{$$p_{i}={\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:48.493Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:48.493Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:31:30.783Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:31:30.783Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:18:27.934Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:18:27.934Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:26:37.641Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:26:37.641Z-->

If the Lagrangian _L_ does _not_ {@{depend on some coordinate _q<sub>i</sub>_}@}, it follows immediately from {@{the Euler–Lagrange equations}@} that {@{$${\dot {p} }_{i}={\frac {\mathrm {d} }{\mathrm {d} t} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }={\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{i} } }=0$$}@} and {@{integrating}@} shows {@{the corresponding generalized momentum equals a constant, a conserved quantity}@}. This is {@{a special case of [Noether's theorem](Noether's%20theorem.md)}@}. Such coordinates are called {@{"cyclic" or "ignorable"}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:28.908Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:28.908Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:33:17.284Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:33:17.284Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:11.088Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:11.088Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:32:08.638Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:32:08.638Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:37.783Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:37.783Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:28.565Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:28.565Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:37:09.468Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:37:09.468Z-->

For example, {@{a system}@} may have {@{a Lagrangian $$L(r,\theta ,{\dot {s} },{\dot {z} },{\dot {r} },{\dot {\theta } },{\dot {\phi } },t),$$}@} where _r_ and _z_ are {@{lengths along straight lines}@}, _s_ is {@{an arc length along some curve}@}, and _θ_ and _φ_ are {@{angles}@}. Notice {@{_z_, _s_, and _φ_}@} are {@{all absent in the Lagrangian even though their velocities are not}@}. Then {@{the momenta $$p_{z}={\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {z} } } },\quad p_{s}={\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {s} } } },\quad p_{\phi }={\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\phi } } } },$$}@} are {@{all conserved quantities}@}. {@{The units and nature of each generalized momentum}@} will depend on {@{the corresponding coordinate}@}; in this case _p_<sub>_z_</sub> is {@{a translational momentum in the _z_ direction}@}, _p_<sub>_s_</sub> is also {@{a translational momentum along the curve _s_ is measured}@}, and _p_<sub>_φ_</sub> is {@{an angular momentum in the plane the angle _φ_ is measured in}@}. {@{However complicated the motion of the system}@} is, {@{all the coordinates and velocities}@} will {@{vary in such a way that these momenta are conserved}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:30:33.866Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:30:33.866Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:22.508Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:22.508Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:17:34.376Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:17:34.376Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:50.346Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:50.346Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:37:40.518Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:37:40.518Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:25:20.826Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:25:20.826Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:09:18.230Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:09:18.230Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:17:47.275Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:17:47.275Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:49.499Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:49.499Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:21.503Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:21.503Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:36.174Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:36.174Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:28:00.477Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:28:00.477Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:49.393Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:49.393Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:59.007Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:59.007Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:15.841Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:15.841Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:36:51.319Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:36:51.319Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:39.649Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:39.649Z-->

### energy

Given {@{a Lagrangian $L$ (annotation: with $V$ being independent of the velocities)}@}, {@{the [Hamiltonian](Hamiltonian%20mechanics.md) of the corresponding mechanical system}@} is, by {@{definition, $$H={\biggl (}\sum _{i=1}^{n}{\dot {q} }_{i}{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }{\biggr )}-L.$$}@} {@{This quantity}@} will be {@{equivalent to energy if the generalized coordinates are natural coordinates}@}, i.e., they have {@{no explicit time dependence when expressing position vector}@}: {@{$\mathbf {r} =\mathbf {r} (q_{1},\cdots ,q_{n})$}@}. From: {@{$$T={\frac {m}{2} }v^{2}={\frac {m}{2} }\sum _{i,j}\left({\frac {\partial {\vec {r} } }{\partial q_{i} } }{\dot {q} }_{i}\right)\cdot \left({\frac {\partial {\vec {r} } }{\partial q_{j} } }{\dot {q} }_{j}\right)={\frac {m}{2} }\sum _{i,j}a_{ij}{\dot {q} }_{i}{\dot {q} }_{j}$$}@} {@{$$\sum _{k=1}^{n}{\dot {q} }_{k}{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{k} } }=\sum _{k=1}^{n}{\dot {q} }_{k}{\frac {\partial T}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{k} } }={\frac {m}{2} }\left(2\sum _{i,j}a_{ij}{\dot {q} }_{i}{\dot {q} }_{j}\right)=2T$$}@} (annotation: We assume {@{the potential energy $V$ is independent of the velocities}@}. For the last equality, consider how many times {@{the matrix $\left[a_{ij}\right]_{i,j=1,\ldots,n}$ is counted over}@}.) {@{$$H=\left(\sum _{i=1}^{n}{\dot {q} }_{i}{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }\right)-L=2T-(T-V)=T+V=E$$}@} where {@{$a_{ij}={\frac {\partial \mathbf {r} }{\partial q_{i} } }\cdot {\frac {\partial \mathbf {r} }{\partial q_{j} } }$}@} is {@{a symmetric matrix that is defined for the derivation}@}. (annotation: This is just {@{the metric tensor of the configuration space}@}.) <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:27:05.950Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:27:05.950Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:57:17.437Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:57:17.437Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:54:04.512Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:54:04.512Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:12.356Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:12.356Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:32.602Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:32.602Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:37:02.357Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:37:02.357Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:00:13.077Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:00:13.077Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:40:00.356Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:40:00.356Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:17.856Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:17.856Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:27.294Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:27.294Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:28:09.362Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:28:09.362Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:23.451Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:23.451Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:14:02.669Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:14:02.669Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:20:57.079Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:20:57.079Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:44:52.620Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:44:52.620Z-->

#### invariance under coordinate transformations

At {@{every time instant _t_}@}, {@{the energy (annotation: with $V$ being independent of the velocities)}@} is {@{invariant under [configuration space](configuration%20space%20(physics).md) coordinate changes __q__ → __Q__}@}, i.e. {@{\(using natural coordinates\) $$E(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } },t)=E(\mathbf {Q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } },t).$$}@} Besides this result, the proof below shows that, under {@{such change of coordinates}@}, {@{the derivatives $\partial L/\partial {\dot {q} }_{i}$}@} change {@{as coefficients of a linear form (annotation: i.e. transforms covariantly)}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:17.753Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:17.753Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:40:22.279Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:40:22.279Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:52:30.473Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:52:30.473Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:25:05.779Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:25:05.779Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:28:26.143Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:28:26.143Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:40.302Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:40.302Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:36:53.980Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:36:53.980Z-->

> __Proof__ (annotation: of {@{invariance of energy under point transformations and covariance of $\frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot{q}_i}$}@})
>
> For {@{a coordinate transformation __Q__ = _F_\(__q__\)}@}, we have {@{$$d\mathbf {Q} =F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )d\mathbf {q} ,$$}@} where {@{$F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )$ is the [tangent map](pushforward%20(differential).md)}@} of {@{the vector space $$\left\{\sum _{i=1}^{n}{\dot {q} }_{i}\cdot \left(\left.{\frac {\partial }{\partial q_{i} } }\right|_{\mathbf {q} }\right)\ {\biggl |}\ {\dot {q} }_{i}\in \mathbb {R} \right\}$$ to the vector space $$\left\{\sum _{i=1}^{n}{\dot {Q} }_{i}\cdot \left(\left.{\frac {\partial }{\partial Q_{i} } }\right|_{F(\mathbf {q} )}\right)\ {\biggl |}\ {\dot {Q} }_{i}\in \mathbb {R} \right\},$$}@} and {@{$$\textstyle F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )=\left(\left.{\frac {\partial F_{i} }{\partial q_{j} } }\right|_{\mathbf {q} }\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}$$}@} is {@{the Jacobian}@}. In {@{the coordinates ${\dot {q} }_{i}$ and ${\dot {Q} }_{i}$}@}, {@{the previous formula for $d\mathbf {Q}$}@} has {@{the form ${\dot {\mathbf {Q} } }=F_{*}(\mathbf {q} ){\dot {\mathbf {q} } }$}@}. (annotation: Both {@{$\mathbf Q$ and $\mathbf q$ can be written as functions of $t$ only}@}, so you can equate {@{the $dt$ component}@}. This form implies {@{$\dot{\mathbf Q}$ is a function of both $\mathbf q$ and $\dot{\mathbf q}$}@}, unlike $\mathbf Q$.) After {@{differentiation involving the product rule}@}, {@{$$d{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } }=G(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } })d\mathbf {q} +F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )d{\dot {\mathbf {q} } },$$}@} where {@{$${\begin{aligned}G(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } })d\mathbf {q} &\,{\stackrel {\text{def} }{=} }\,d(F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )){\dot {\mathbf {q} } }=\left(\sum _{k=1}^{n}{\frac {\partial ^{2}F_{i} }{\partial q_{j}\partial q_{k} } }{\biggl |}_{\mathbf {q} }dq_{k}\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}{\dot {\mathbf {q} } }=\left(\sum _{j=1}^{n}{\dot {q} }_{j}\sum _{k=1}^{n}{\frac {\partial ^{2}F_{i} }{\partial q_{j}\partial q_{k} } }{\biggl |}_{\mathbf {q} }dq_{k}\right)_{i=1,\ldots ,n}^{T}\\&=\left(\sum _{k=1}^{n}dq_{k}\sum _{j=1}^{n}{\frac {\partial ^{2}F_{i} }{\partial q_{j}\partial q_{k} } }{\biggl |}_{\mathbf {q} }{\dot {q} }_{j}\right)_{i=1,\ldots ,n}^{T}=\left(\sum _{j=1}^{n}{\frac {\partial ^{2}F_{i} }{\partial q_{j}\partial q_{k} } }{\biggl |}_{\mathbf {q} }{\dot {q} }_{j}\right)_{i,k=1}^{n}d\mathbf {q} .\end{aligned} }$$}@} (annotation: To {@{understand the algebraic manipulation}@}, just realize that we are simply {@{manipulating matrices and vectors}@} here.)
>
> In {@{vector notation}@}, {@{$${\begin{aligned}dL(\mathbf {Q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } },t)&={\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {Q} } }d\mathbf {Q} +{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {Q} } } } }d{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } }+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t} }dt\\&=\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {Q} } }F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {Q} } } } }G(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } })\right)d\mathbf {q} +{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {Q} } } } }F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )d{\dot {\mathbf {q} } }+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t} }.\end{aligned} }$$}@}
>
> On the other hand, {@{$$dL(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } },t)={\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {q} } }d\mathbf {q} +{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {q} } } } }d{\dot {\mathbf {q} } }+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t} }dt.$$}@}
>
> It was mentioned earlier that {@{Lagrangians do not depend on the choice of configuration space coordinates}@}, i.e. {@{$L(\mathbf {Q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } },t)=L(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } },t)$}@}. One implication of this is that {@{$dL(\mathbf {Q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {Q} } },t)=dL(\mathbf {q} ,{\dot {\mathbf {q} } },t)$}@}, and {@{$${\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {Q} } } } }F_{*}(\mathbf {q} )={\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {q} } } } }.$$}@} (annotation: Equate {@{the $d\dot{\mathbf q}$ component above}@}.) This demonstrates that, for each {@{$\mathbf {q}$, ${\dot {\mathbf {q} } }$, and $t$}@}, {@{$\textstyle \sum \limits _{i=1}^{n}{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }d{\dot {q} }_{i}$}@} is {@{a well-defined linear form whose coefficients $\textstyle {\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }$ are covariant 1-tensors (annotation: the (transposed) Jacobian converts in reverse from $\mathbf Q$ to $\mathbf q$)}@}. Applying {@{(annotation: i.e. multiplying) both sides of the equation to ${\dot {\mathbf {q} } }$}@} and using {@{the above formula for ${\dot {\mathbf {Q} } }$ (annotation: $\dot{\mathbf Q} = F_*(\mathbf q) \dot{\mathbf q}$)}@} yields {@{$${\dot {\mathbf {Q} } }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {Q} } } } }={\dot {\mathbf {q} } }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {\mathbf {q} } } } }.$$}@} {@{The invariance of the energy $E$}@} follows (annotation: recall {@{the definition of the Hamiltonian}@}). <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:41:58.110Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:41:58.110Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:10:08.768Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:10:08.768Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:34.905Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:34.905Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:25:02.496Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:25:02.496Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:10.931Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:10.931Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:24.146Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:24.146Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:58.450Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:58.450Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:54.178Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:54.178Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:31:01.588Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:31:01.588Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:24:15.223Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:24:15.223Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:27.371Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:27.371Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:21:13.203Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:21:13.203Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:51:42.135Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:51:42.135Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:33.813Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:33.813Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:11:08.010Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:11:08.010Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:10.847Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:10.847Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:13:15.992Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:13:15.992Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:38.258Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:38.258Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:57:45.701Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:57:45.701Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:39:13.990Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:39:13.990Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:48.120Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:48.120Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:56.852Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:56.852Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:57:01.933Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:57:01.933Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:25:04.780Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:25:04.780Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:41:54.674Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:41:54.674Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:40.974Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:40.974Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:24.586Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:24.586Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:33.269Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:33.269Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T04:28:23.084Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T04:28:23.084Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:28:26.256Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:28:26.256Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:31.249Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:31.249Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:43.320Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:43.320Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:17:28.855Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:17:28.855Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:34.098Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:34.098Z-->

#### conservation

In {@{Lagrangian mechanics}@}, {@{the system is [closed](closed%20system.md)}@} {@{if and only if its Lagrangian $L$ does not explicitly depend on time}@}. {@{The [energy conservation law](energy%20conservation%20law.md)}@} states that {@{the energy $E$ of a closed system is an [integral of motion](integral%20of%20motion.md#integral%20of%20motion)}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:47.732Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:47.732Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:35.244Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:35.244Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:52.724Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:52.724Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:27:48.828Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:27:48.828Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:43:32.683Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:43:32.683Z-->

More precisely, let {@{__q__ = __q__\(_t_\) be an _extremal_}@}. \(In other words, __q__ {@{satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equations}@}\). Taking {@{the total time-derivative of _L_ along this extremal and using the EL equations}@} leads to {@{$${\begin{aligned}{\frac {dL}{dt} }&={\dot {\mathbf {q} } }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {q} } }+{\ddot {\mathbf {q} } }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {\dot {q} } } }+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t} }\\-{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t} }&={\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {\dot {q} } } }\right){\dot {\mathbf {q} } }+{\ddot {\mathbf {q} } }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {\dot {q} } } }-{\dot {L} }\\-{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t} }&={\frac {d}{dt} }\left({\frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {\dot {q} } } }\mathbf {\dot {q} } -L\right)={\frac {dH}{dt} }\end{aligned} }$$}@} (annotation: {@{The second to third line}@} uses {@{the product rule}@}.) <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:23.112Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:23.112Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:41:51.472Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:41:51.472Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:50:02.617Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:50:02.617Z!fsrs,2026-08-29T06:57:29.850Z,12,11.6874828,1,2,2,0,0,2026-08-17T06:57:29.850Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:56:40.739Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:56:40.739Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:04.664Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:04.664Z-->

If {@{the Lagrangian _L_ does not explicitly depend on time}@}, then {@{∂<!-- markdown separator -->_L_<!-- markdown separator -->/∂<!-- markdown separator -->_t_ = 0}@}, then {@{_H_ does not vary with time evolution of particle, indeed, an integral of motion}@}, meaning that {@{$$H(\mathbf {q} (t),{\dot {\mathbf {q} } }(t),t)={\text{constant of time} }.$$}@} Hence, if {@{the chosen coordinates were natural coordinates}@}, {@{the energy is conserved}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:32.103Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:32.103Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:32:53.407Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:32:53.407Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:32:06.745Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:32:06.745Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:43:34.275Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:43:34.275Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:32.375Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:32.375Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:36:37.157Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:36:37.157Z-->

#### kinetic and potential energies

Under {@{all these circumstances (annotation: both the generalized coordinates and $L$ do not have explicit time dependence)}@},<sup>[\[41\]](#^ref-41)</sup> {@{the constant $$E=T+V$$ is the total energy of the system}@}. {@{The kinetic and potential energies}@} still {@{change as the system evolves}@}, but {@{the motion of the system}@} will be such that {@{their sum, the total energy, is constant}@}. This is {@{a valuable simplification}@}, since {@{the energy _E_ is a constant of integration}@} that counts as {@{an arbitrary constant for the problem}@}, and it may be possible to integrate {@{the velocities from this energy relation to solve for the coordinates}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:43:52.149Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:43:52.149Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:09:12.393Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:09:12.393Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:22.893Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:22.893Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:08:27.403Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:08:27.403Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:22.254Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:22.254Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:40:19.136Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:40:19.136Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:43.564Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:43.564Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:26.670Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:26.670Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:47:31.926Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:47:31.926Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T04:29:53.958Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T04:29:53.958Z-->

### mechanical similarity

- Main article: ::@:: [Mechanical similarity](mechanical%20similarity.md) <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:16.495Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:16.495Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:31:13.926Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:31:13.926Z-->

If {@{the potential energy}@} is {@{a [homogeneous function](homogeneous%20function.md) of the coordinates and independent of time}@},<sup>[\[42\]](#^ref-42)</sup> and {@{all position vectors are scaled by the same nonzero constant _α_}@}, {@{__r__<sub>_k_</sub>′ = _α_<!-- markdown separator -->__r__<sub>_k_</sub>}@}, so that {@{$$V(\alpha \mathbf {r} _{1},\alpha \mathbf {r} _{2},\ldots ,\alpha \mathbf {r} _{N})=\alpha ^{N}V(\mathbf {r} _{1},\mathbf {r} _{2},\ldots ,\mathbf {r} _{N})$$}@} and {@{time is scaled by a factor _β_}@}, {@{_t_′ = _βt_}@}, then {@{the velocities __v__<sub>_k_</sub>}@} are scaled by {@{a factor of _α_<!-- markdown separator -->/<!-- markdown separator -->_β_}@} and {@{the kinetic energy _T_ by \(_α_<!-- markdown separator -->/<!-- markdown separator -->_β_\)<sup>2</sup>}@}. {@{The entire Lagrangian has been scaled by the same factor (annotation: giving the same equations of motion)}@} if {@{$${\frac {\alpha ^{2} }{\beta ^{2} } }=\alpha ^{N}\quad \Rightarrow \quad \beta =\alpha ^{1-{\frac {N}{2} } }.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:17:30.927Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:17:30.927Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:57:02.710Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:57:02.710Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:34:48.049Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:34:48.049Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:38:07.543Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:38:07.543Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:21:17.203Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:21:17.203Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:41:14.888Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:41:14.888Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:14:09.927Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:14:09.927Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:47.851Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:47.851Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:34:49.726Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:34:49.726Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:42:23.686Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:42:23.686Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:50:32.030Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:50:32.030Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T05:59:32.222Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T05:59:32.222Z-->

Since {@{the lengths and times have been scaled}@}, {@{the trajectories of the particles}@} in the system follow {@{geometrically similar paths differing in size}@}. {@{The length _l_ traversed in time _t_ in the original trajectory}@} corresponds to {@{a new length _l_′ traversed in time _t_′ in the new trajectory}@}, given by the ratios {@{$${\frac {t'}{t} }=\left({\frac {l'}{l} }\right)^{1-{\frac {N}{2} } }.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:33.679Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:33.679Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T17:52:31.316Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T17:52:31.316Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:35:52.239Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:35:52.239Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:27:46.436Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:27:46.436Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T06:24:16.242Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T06:24:16.242Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:34:55.069Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:34:55.069Z-->

### interacting particles

For {@{a given system}@}, if {@{two subsystems _A_ and _B_ are non-interacting}@}, {@{the Lagrangian _L_ of the overall system}@} is {@{the sum of the Lagrangians _L_<sub>_A_</sub> and _L_<sub>_B_</sub> for the subsystems}@}:<sup>[\[37\]](#^ref-37)</sup> {@{$$L=L_{A}+L_{B}.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:52:59.545Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:52:59.545Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:30:08.771Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:30:08.771Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:48:34.559Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:48:34.559Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:44:47.482Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:44:47.482Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:00:05.280Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:00:05.280Z-->

If they do {@{interact this is not possible}@}. In {@{some situations}@}, it may be possible to {@{separate the Lagrangian of the system _L_ into the sum of non-interacting Lagrangians}@}, plus {@{another Lagrangian _L_<sub>_AB_</sub> containing information about the interaction}@}, {@{$$L=L_{A}+L_{B}+L_{AB}.$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:17:48.624Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:17:48.624Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:53:36.834Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:53:36.834Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:49:57.299Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:49:57.299Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:49:12.760Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:49:12.760Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:36:55.746Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:36:55.746Z-->

This may be {@{physically motivated}@} by taking {@{the non-interacting Lagrangians to be kinetic energies only}@}, while {@{the interaction Lagrangian is the system's total potential energy}@}. Also, in {@{the limiting case of negligible interaction}@}, {@{_L_<sub>_AB_</sub> tends to zero}@} reducing to {@{the non-interacting case above}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:28:10.040Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:28:10.040Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:38.090Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:38.090Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:24:24.652Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:24:24.652Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:35:49.713Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:35:49.713Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T18:18:29.343Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T18:18:29.343Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:03:42.424Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:03:42.424Z-->

{@{The extension to more than two non-interacting subsystems}@} is {@{straightforward}@} – {@{the overall Lagrangian}@} is {@{the sum of the separate Lagrangians for each subsystem}@}. If {@{there are interactions}@}, then {@{interaction Lagrangians may be added}@}. <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:06.751Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:06.751Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:40:21.397Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:40:21.397Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:36:52.894Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:36:52.894Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:25:38.523Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:25:38.523Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:39:01.513Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:39:01.513Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:56:09.791Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:56:09.791Z-->

### consequences of singular Largrangians

From {@{the Euler-Lagrange equations}@}, it follows that: {@{$${\begin{aligned}&{\frac {d}{dt} }{\frac {\partial L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }-{\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{i} } }=0\\&\sum_j {\frac {\partial ^{2}L}{\partial q_{j}\partial {\dot {q} }_{i} } }{\frac {dq_{j} }{dt} }+\sum_j {\frac {\partial ^{2}L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{j}\partial {\dot q}_{i} } }{\frac {d{\dot {q} }_{j} }{dt} }+{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t \partial {\dot q}_{i} } }-{\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{i} } }=0\\&\sum _{j}W_{ij}(q,{\dot {q} },t){\ddot {q} }_{j}={\frac {\partial L}{\partial q_{i} } }-{\frac {\partial L}{\partial t \partial {\dot q}_i} }-\sum _{j}{\frac {\partial ^{2}L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i}\partial q_{j} } }{\dot {q} }_{j},\\\end{aligned} }$$}@} where the matrix is defined as {@{$W_{ij}={\frac {\partial ^{2}L}{\partial {\dot {q} }_{i}\partial {\dot {q} }_{j} } }$}@}. If {@{the matrix $W$ is non-singular}@}, {@{the above equations}@} can be {@{solved to represent ${\ddot {q} }$ as a function of $({\dot {q} },q,t)$}@}. If {@{the matrix is non-invertible}@}, it would not be {@{possible to represent all ${\ddot {q} }$<!-- markdown separator -->'s as a function of $({\dot {q} },q,t)$}@} but also, {@{the Hamiltonian equations of motions}@} will {@{not take the standard form (annotation: the standard form is ${\dot q}_i = \frac{\partial H}{\partial p_i}$ and ${\dot p}_i = -\frac{\partial H}{\partial q_i}$)}@}.<sup>[\[43\]](#^ref-43)</sup> <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:48:39.144Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:48:39.144Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:43:19.894Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:43:19.894Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:31:12.344Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:31:12.344Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:27:35.278Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:27:35.278Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:33:49.935Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:33:49.935Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T23:37:11.714Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T23:37:11.714Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:50:36.700Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:50:36.700Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:27:39.871Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:27:39.871Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T01:02:12.674Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T01:02:12.674Z!fsrs,2026-08-23T20:34:28.131Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-15T20:34:28.131Z-->

## examples

{@{The following examples}@} apply {@{Lagrange's equations of the second kind}@} to {@{mechanical problems}@}. (annotation: 4 items: {@{conservative force, pendulum on a movable support, two-body central force problem}@}) <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T00:29:37.669Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T00:29:37.669Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:54:06.117Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:54:06.117Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T22:13:20.249Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T22:13:20.249Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:07.930Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:07.930Z-->

### conservative force

{@{A particle of mass _m_}@} moves under {@{the influence of a [conservative force](conservative%20force.md)}@} derived from {@{the [gradient](gradient.md) ∇ of a [scalar potential](scalar%20potential.md)}@}, {@{$$\mathbf {F} =-{\boldsymbol {\nabla } }V(\mathbf {r} ).$$}@} <!--SR:!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:42:31.348Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:42:31.348Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:55:17.441Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:55:17.441Z!fsrs,2026-08-25T06:38:31.508Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-17T06:38:31.508Z!fsrs,2026-08-24T06:07:25.615Z,8,8.2956,1,2,1,0,0,2026-08-16T06:07:25.615Z-->
